It's like my brain just checks out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Not really anxious, no. I don't feel nervous or anything like that while I'm there. And I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed by the work itself, either, because I know how to do all the steps. It's just... I just can't keep my attention on it. It's not a stressed feeling, just kind of a blank one.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or completing tasks outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed it being an issue outside of work. I mean, I live alone, so if I don't finish folding laundry right away or whatever, it doesn't really matter. Work is where it's actually causing problems for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I'll start a shift trying really hard to stay locked in and do everything right. But after a while, all the cutting, weighing, and checking labels... it just sort of blurs together. Next thing I know, I realize I’ve been staring at the same tray or the same log sheet for a few minutes, and I'm not even taking in what I’m supposed to do next. It's like my brain just checks out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Not really anxious, no. I don't feel nervous or anything like that while I'm there. And I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed by the work itself, either, because I know how to do all the steps. It's just... I just can't keep my attention on it. It's not a stressed feeling, just kind of a blank one.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or completing tasks outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed it being an issue outside of work. I mean, I live alone, so if I don't finish folding laundry right away or whatever, it doesn't really matter. Work is where it's actually causing problems for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not working?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless or like I need to be moving around all the time. I can sit still just fine when I'm at home watching TV or something. It's really just the focus part that's the issue, not feeling fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or depressed or anything like that. My mood is generally fine. I mean, I get frustrated that I can't keep my focus at work, but I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most of the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yes, I did. My mom actually still has some of my old elementary school report cards, like from around fourth grade, I think. It's like my brain just checks out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Not really anxious, no. I don't feel nervous or anything like that while I'm there. And I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed by the work itself, either, because I know how to do all the steps. It's just... I just can't keep my attention on it. It's not a stressed feeling, just kind of a blank one.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or completing tasks outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed it being an issue outside of work. I mean, I live alone, so if I don't finish folding laundry right away or whatever, it doesn't really matter. Work is where it's actually causing problems for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not working?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless or like I need to be moving around all the time. I can sit still just fine when I'm at home watching TV or something. It's really just the focus part that's the issue, not feeling fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or depressed or anything like that. My mood is generally fine. I mean, I get frustrated that I can't keep my focus at work, but I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing at work for a short time or has this been an issue for a while?\n\nPatient: It's been a while, honestly. This has been a serious problem since I switched into full-time meat processing, which was about... two years and four months ago, I think. It's just been a struggle ever since I started doing that full-time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the go or have a lot of extra energy when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. It's kind of like what I was saying earlier about not feeling fidgety. I really don't have a bunch of extra energy, and I definitely don't feel like I'm constantly on the go. When I'm off the clock, I'm pretty normal in that regard... I just like to relax. So no, that's not something I experience.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life events or changes around the time your focus problems at work started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Aside from switching to the full-time meat processing job, nothing else major happened around that time. It was just the job change, really.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or finishing tasks when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. My mom actually still has some of my old elementary school report cards, like from around fourth grade, I think. The teachers would always write that I was bright, but that I needed constant reminders to actually finish my classwork and turn in my homework. I remember I would understand the lessons fine, but I'd just... miss big chunks of the instructions unless the teacher was right there making sure I was paying attention. So yeah, it's been an issue for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotionally bothered by your inability to focus at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I get upset or emotionally bothered by it. I mean, it's definitely frustrating when I fall behind or mess something up, but I don't really get... down about it, I guess. It's just more of a practical issue for me. I just want to figure out how to get my work done properly without so much hassle, you know? So no, it doesn't really take an emotional toll on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be putting off certain tasks at work because you don't like doing them?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not exactly that I don't like doing them... it's more about the type of task. Like, the physical part of the job is fine, but I definitely put off the certification modules. And any of the long procedure reading, I just avoid it until the absolute last possible minute. Sitting there, trying to focus and actually absorb all those safety rules and regulations... it just takes so much out of me. It honestly feels almost physically exhausting to force myself to read through all that text.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just keep starting my tasks, like the sanitation steps, the batch logs, or the end-of-line paperwork, but I somehow always leave out the last parts. \n\nIt's really becoming a problem. I mean, I know my job depends on finishing everything correctly, but I just can't seem to follow through and actually complete the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're forgetting to do the last parts of your tasks or do you just lose interest or get distracted?\n\nPatient: I think it's more about getting distracted or just losing focus. I'll start a shift trying really hard to stay locked in and do everything right. But after a while, all the cutting, weighing, and checking labels... it just sort of blurs together. Next thing I know, I realize I’ve been staring at the same tray or the same log sheet for a few minutes, and I'm not even taking in what I’m supposed to do next. It's like my brain just checks out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Not really anxious, no. I don't feel nervous or anything like that while I'm there. And I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed by the work itself, either, because I know how to do all the steps. It's just... I just can't keep my attention on it. It's not a stressed feeling, just kind of a blank one.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or completing tasks outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed it being an issue outside of work. I mean, I live alone, so if I don't finish folding laundry right away or whatever, it doesn't really matter. Work is where it's actually causing problems for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not working?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless or like I need to be moving around all the time. I can sit still just fine when I'm at home watching TV or something. It's really just the focus part that's the issue, not feeling fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or depressed or anything like that. My mood is generally fine. I mean, I get frustrated that I can't keep my focus at work, but I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing at work for a short time or has this been an issue for a while?\n\nPatient: It's been a while, honestly. This has been a serious problem since I switched into full-time meat processing, which was about... two years and four months ago, I think. It's just been a struggle ever since I started doing that full-time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the go or have a lot of extra energy when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. It's kind of like what I was saying earlier about not feeling fidgety. I really don't have a bunch of extra energy, and I definitely don't feel like I'm constantly on the go. So no, that's not something I experience.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life events or changes around the time your focus problems at work started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It was just the job change, really.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or finishing tasks when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. My mom actually still has some of my old elementary school report cards, like from around fourth grade, I think. The teachers would always write that I was bright, but that I needed constant reminders to actually finish my classwork and turn in my homework. I remember I would understand the lessons fine, but I'd just... miss big chunks of the instructions unless the teacher was right there making sure I was paying attention. So yeah, it's been an issue for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotionally bothered by your inability to focus at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I get upset or emotionally bothered by it. I mean, it's definitely frustrating when I fall behind or mess something up, but I don't really get... down about it, I guess. It's just more of a practical issue for me. I just want to figure out how to get my work done properly without so much hassle, you know? So no, it doesn't really take an emotional toll on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be putting off certain tasks at work because you don't like doing them?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not exactly that I don't like doing them... it's more about the type of task. Like, the physical part of the job is fine, but I definitely put off the certification modules. And any of the long procedure reading, I just avoid it until the absolute last possible minute. Sitting there, trying to focus and actually absorb all those safety rules and regulations... it just takes so much out of me. It honestly feels almost physically exhausting to force myself to read through all that text.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble focusing at work because you're trying to avoid doing certain tasks or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on them regardless of what they are?\n\nPatient: I think it's more that I just can't keep my attention on them, regardless of what it is. Like, I can start my shift trying really hard to stay focused and locked in on what I'm doing. But after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking labels... it all just kind of blurs together. I'll suddenly realize I've been staring at the same tray of meat or the same log sheet for a few minutes, and I'm not even taking in what I'm supposed to be doing next. So it's not really about avoiding the work, my brain just sort of checks out on its own.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The teachers would always write that I was bright, but that I needed constant reminders to actually finish my classwork and turn in my homework. I remember I would understand the lessons fine, but I'd just... miss big chunks of the instructions unless the teacher was right there making sure I was paying attention. So yeah, it's been an issue for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotionally bothered by your inability to focus at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I get upset or emotionally bothered by it. I mean, it's definitely frustrating when I fall behind or mess something up, but I don't really get... down about it, I guess. It's just more of a practical issue for me. I just want to figure out how to get my work done properly without so much hassle, you know? So no, it doesn't really take an emotional toll on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be putting off certain tasks at work because you don't like doing them?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not exactly that I don't like doing them... it's more about the type of task. Like, the physical part of the job is fine, but I definitely put off the certification modules. And any of the long procedure reading, I just avoid it until the absolute last possible minute. Sitting there, trying to focus and actually absorb all those safety rules and regulations... it just takes so much out of me. It honestly feels almost physically exhausting to force myself to read through all that text.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble focusing at work because you're trying to avoid doing certain tasks or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on them regardless of what they are?\n\nPatient: I think it's more that I just can't keep my attention on them, regardless of what it is. Like, I can start my shift trying really hard to stay focused and locked in on what I'm doing. But after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking labels... it all just kind of blurs together. I'll suddenly realize I've been staring at the same tray of meat or the same log sheet for a few minutes, and I'm not even taking in what I'm supposed to be doing next. So it's not really about avoiding the work, my brain just sort of checks out on its own.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel emotionally drained or upset after realizing you've lost focus at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I mentioned before, I don't really get upset or emotionally bothered by it. It's frustrating, sure, but I don't feel emotionally drained or down about it when I realize my mind wandered. It's just more of an annoyance because it slows me down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I mean, I know my job depends on finishing everything correctly, but I just can't seem to follow through and actually complete the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're forgetting to do the last parts of your tasks or do you just lose interest or get distracted?\n\nPatient: I think it's more about getting distracted or just losing focus. I'll start a shift trying really hard to stay locked in and do everything right. But after a while, all the cutting, weighing, and checking labels... it just sort of blurs together. Next thing I know, I realize I’ve been staring at the same tray or the same log sheet for a few minutes, and I'm not even taking in what I’m supposed to do next. It's like my brain just checks out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Not really anxious, no. I don't feel nervous or anything like that while I'm there. And I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed by the work itself, either, because I know how to do all the steps. It's just... I just can't keep my attention on it. It's not a stressed feeling, just kind of a blank one.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or completing tasks outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed it being an issue outside of work. I mean, I live alone, so if I don't finish folding laundry right away or whatever, it doesn't really matter. Work is where it's actually causing problems for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not working?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless or like I need to be moving around all the time. I can sit still just fine when I'm at home watching TV or something. This has been a serious problem since I switched into full-time meat processing, which was about... two years and four months ago, I think. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because I'm having a lot of trouble at work lately. I work at a meat processing plant, and... I just keep starting my tasks, like the sanitation steps, the batch logs, or the end-of-line paperwork, but I somehow always leave out the last parts. \n\nIt's really becoming a problem. I mean, I know my job depends on finishing everything correctly, but I just can't seem to follow through and actually complete the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're forgetting to do the last parts of your tasks or do you just lose interest or get distracted?\n\nPatient: I think it's more about getting distracted or just losing focus. I'll start a shift trying really hard to stay locked in and do everything right. But after a while, all the cutting, weighing, and checking labels... it just sort of blurs together. Next thing I know, I realize I’ve been staring at the same tray or the same log sheet for a few minutes, and I'm not even taking in what I’m supposed to do next. It's like my brain just checks out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Not really anxious, no. I don't feel nervous or anything like that while I'm there. And I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed by the work itself, either, because I know how to do all the steps. It's just... I just can't keep my attention on it. It's not a stressed feeling, just kind of a blank one.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or completing tasks outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I haven't really noticed it being an issue outside of work. I mean, I live alone, so if I don't finish folding laundry right away or whatever, it doesn't really matter. Work is where it's actually causing problems for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not working?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless or like I need to be moving around all the time. I can sit still just fine when I'm at home watching TV or something. It's really just the focus part that's the issue, not feeling fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or depressed or anything like that. My mood is generally fine. I mean, I get frustrated that I can't keep my focus at work, but I wouldn't say I'm feeling down most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing at work for a short time or has this been an issue for a while?\n\nPatient: It's been a while, honestly. This has been a serious problem since I switched into full-time meat processing, which was about... two years and four months ago, I think. It's just been a struggle ever since I started doing that full-time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the go or have a lot of extra energy when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. It's kind of like what I was saying earlier about not feeling fidgety. I really don't have a bunch of extra energy, and I definitely don't feel like I'm constantly on the go. When I'm off the clock, I'm pretty normal in that regard... I just like to relax. So no, that's not something I experience.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life events or changes around the time your focus problems at work started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Aside from switching to the full-time meat processing job, nothing else major happened around that time. It was just the job change, really.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or finishing tasks when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. My mom actually still has some of my old elementary school report cards, like from around fourth grade, I think. The teachers would always write that I was bright, but that I needed constant reminders to actually finish my classwork and turn in my homework. I remember I would understand the lessons fine, but I'd just... miss big chunks of the instructions unless the teacher was right there making sure I was paying attention. So yeah, it's been an issue for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotionally bothered by your inability to focus at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I get upset or emotionally bothered by it. I mean, it's definitely frustrating when I fall behind or mess something up, but I don't really get... down about it, I guess. It's just more of a practical issue for me. I just want to figure out how to get my work done properly without so much hassle, you know? So no, it doesn't really take an emotional toll on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be putting off certain tasks at work because you don't like doing them?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not exactly that I don't like doing them... it's more about the type of task. Like, the physical part of the job is fine, but I definitely put off the certification modules. And any of the long procedure reading, I just avoid it until the absolute last possible minute. Sitting there, trying to focus and actually absorb all those safety rules and regulations... it just takes so much out of me. It honestly feels almost physically exhausting to force myself to read through all that text.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble focusing at work because you're trying to avoid doing certain tasks or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on them regardless of what they are?\n\nPatient: I think it's more that I just can't keep my attention on them, regardless of what it is. Like, I can start my shift trying really hard to stay focused and locked in on what I'm doing. But after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking labels... it all just kind of blurs together. I'll suddenly realize I've been staring at the same tray of meat or the same log sheet for a few minutes, and I'm not even taking in what I'm supposed to be doing next. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm not really sure. It's hard to say because, like I mentioned, I haven't had another job since I graduated to compare it to. Maybe if I was doing something that changed more day to day, it wouldn't be as bad. But I honestly don't know if the focus issues would just follow me there anyway.
